ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e2965	Good morning, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! My question, Mrs Minister, is: 42/M “How do you justify the postponement of health reform to 2009 in view of the precarious financial situation of the local health phone?”
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e2987	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e3002	A wonderful morning! Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Values listeners! Mrs Oberhauser, thank you very much for this question, because it allows me to give an overview. At the beginning, I would like to point out that this is by no means a delay, but that, at the beginning of this legislative period, my department has planned all major projects project management by project and also provided for appropriate procedures for each undertaking. So it was already planned from the beginning of 2007 that we will be able to take the next big step in 2009 with the end of 2008, where very, very many projects that my predecessor has already begun. But let me say something: It is essential to distinguish between acute financial problems and the definition of health reform. A health reform does not mean in my opinion and in our eyes only a financial contribution, but a health reform involves many structural changes within this health care system. That is why we have divided the problem into three areas: We are talking about a short-, medium- and long-term solution. The short-term problem solution concerns primarily the Vienna regional health insurance fund, which has come into a very precarious financial situation from many different situations. The responsible persons of the Vienna regional health insurance fund have referred to this precarious situation, their responsibility accordingly. Due to a meeting with the Federal Chancellor, the Minister of Finance, the Minister of Health and the responsible members of the main association, there are the solutions for the acute situation of the Wiener Landeskrankenkasse, namely, in the form of support from the main association of payments to be made in the context of hospital financing and, of course, also by the moderate increase in contributions by 0.15 percent. There are continuous discussions between the responsible representatives of the Wiener Gebietskrankenkasse, the main association, the Ministry of Health and the Chancellor's Office responsible as a supervisory body to secure the financial resources for 2008. The second area – and I think it is very, very essential – is that not only the Vienna regional health insurance fund, but also other local health telephones have a financial problem that there are increasingly shortages in the cost-calculation preview. Therefore, it was a clear wish that, together with the main association, including in its self-management sector, concepts should be developed for the next phase to find a way to reduce these cost increases accordingly, respectfully ensure the financing of these structures. A paper from the social partners was prepared to be handed over to me before Easter, and I will work through this paper together with the social partners on Easter from the political side. We have already given the chancellor the signal that we ask him to invite him back to this round in the first week after Easter to decide on the next steps in the medium term. Health reform in itself is a structural reform and is used to ensure this system's sustainability and long-term security. You know that the first steps have already been taken by my predecessor. It is about merging the dual financing system in Austria into planning, management and financing from a single source. In order to achieve this final result and goal, with the best possible medical support for all Austrians, very many framework projects were necessary to enable structural change. The main issue here is the question of the performance-oriented funding of hospitals, which has so far been exclusively related to the stationary sector. By the end of the year, a working group of the Ministry has proposed extending this performance-oriented financing system to the outpatient and day-care sectors. However, in line with the agreements in the financial compensatory negotiations, it is also a matter of creating medical care close to the place of residence, of creating health centres in the area nearby, for which legal conditions are created together with the Austrian Medical Chamber by the end of the year. However, it is also – and this is very important – the link between the hospital and the lowered area, in particular, to present the so-called regional structural plans health, in the implementation in the countries, by the end of the year by the countries with a compatibility between the stationary and the outpatient sector. Many federal states are already on a very good path in this regard. Finally, this also concerns the issue of quality standards for individual health services. There are several Court of Auditors reports on this area – I recall the Court of Auditors' report on intra-mural institutions last year – which show where a very strong cost improvement is possible without restriction on performance. However, there are also Court of Auditors reports which show that different structures of the regional health telephone can be identified in comparison. That is to say, this is a long-term project, which has been planned accordingly and primarily includes the structural situation.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e3619	Mrs Bundesminister, my question is: 45/M “How do you want to ensure quality assurance in the health sector?”
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e3641	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e3656	Deputies! It is of course health policy's task to develop health care in every way and to ensure that there is a balance between health services, financing, but above all quality assurance. I would point out that the Austrian health system, in international comparison, receives excellent quality, performance assessments and that we have over 40 laws in this area that guarantee quality assurance in health care. There are provisions here. Medical equipment law, blood safety law, medical law, but also, of course, the agreements between the federal government and the countries. In my opinion, however, it is a very essential step, which is currently being worked on in many working groups, that quality is also related to bringing in paths and structures in medical procedures to create guidelines. Many institutions are working to take account of the fact that every patient, every patient in Austria, has the opportunity to receive treatment with the same quality approach wherever and in which institution he or she is treated. Think of the Austrian osteoporosis report, which is catastrophic, which contains terrifying numbers! In reality, however, this osteoporosis report does not explain who can be sent to an additional investigation at any time with what predisposition or what family stress. And so it can happen that a young woman is sent by a gynecologist to a bone density measurement, so that in the end – let me say provocatively – equipment utilizations also take place, while on the other hand a woman with two hip fractures in the six-week range is not given a corresponding examination. That is to say, these guidelines are an essential element of quality. However, in order to institutionalize the quality – and this is one of the key objectives of my department – we have opened up the Federal Institute for Quality Assurance in Health Care in the middle of last year, and here I see a great opportunity to provide, in addition to the development of tools for quality measurement, so-called structural quality criteria, i.e. with regard to the medical services provided, to also ask: Under what criteria, with what professionals and also in what number does a medical service have to be performed in order to take into account the quality of the patient as well as the patient? This is also an essential element of control mechanisms in order to postpone performance here, to move beyond the sectoral boundaries, and it is very welcome that the financial compensatory negotiations that we conducted last year gave the Ministry of Health as a senior federal organisation quality control not only in the intramural area, but also in the extramural sector, which also guarantees the combination of the quality criteria for people accordingly.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e8585	Mr President. High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! On this issue – Austria/Czech Republic on nuclear safety, the implementation of a nuclear information agreement – one must correct a few things that were also mentioned here in the debate, because they were not presented accordingly. What is completely forgotten is the fact that years ago, in the Brussels agreement, at the beginning of this dialogue, this process, we agreed that expert discussions should be started to amend the bilateral nuclear information agreement. What we are discussing here today is therefore the fulfilment of a contract under the Brussels agreement. Whoever says this today, we do not need it and this is not useful, it leaves out an essential point of this debate and weakens our position in the drafting of the Brussels agreement, which at least we want to keep our point in mind. That is why we have also developed and discussed the Nuclear Information Agreement, and that is what is available here today. It is also necessary to know that the entire implementation of the Road Map for the Brussels agreement has taken place within the framework of the Nuclear Information Agreement. Everyone in this House has repeatedly insisted that the Road Map is to be dealt with on the spot, and we have done so and developed it within the Nuclear Information Agreement. The amended Agreement does not replace the Brussels Agreement. Negotiations have lasted for a very long time – admittedly – but – and I am glad that we have taken a long time – it contains significant improvements, and I want to put them here too, because no word has been lost about quality improvements at all. This information agreement extends the general obligation to provide information to events where it is necessary to take account of the information needs of the people of the neighbouring country. In addition, we can actively request further explanations and additional information with the new agreement. This has not yet been possible within the framework of the agreement. Second point: The exchange of information on nuclear installations is significantly expanded because not only are major changes, such as the improvement of performance, renewal or expansion of an operating permit, but also the decommissioning and reconstruction of nuclear installations are now being recorded. Third point: It also established its own legal basis for extensive consultations in individual cases. Fourth point – and I think this is important in a debate that is now and there in the Czech Republic – is: In the event that the Czech Republic is actually planning or building further nuclear power plants, it has been and will be put on a completely new basis to this consultation. In future, information must be provided and exchanged not only in the context of the construction permit, but already at the planning stage. This is a qualitative improvement on the issue of the Nuclear Information Agreement. ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e9729	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Mr Lichtenecker, it would be better to stick to the items on the agenda than to spread horror scenarios here about really necessary waste incineration plants. But now on the less emotional battery directive of the European Union. – In addition, there is sufficient provision here to ensure that you can collect as you can from my remarks. What are the main points of this directive? – The use of dangerous substances in batteries and accumulators is restricted. All batteries are to be collected and recycled at the end of their useful life. Batteries are now also distinguished in categories: Device batteries, vehicle batteries and industrial batteries. The devices shall be designed in such a way that the batteries are removable and the battery capacity is attached to the device. In addition, collection targets of 25 and 45 percent of the average sales of the last three years are also set. The collected batteries must be able to be used in a material way. In order to finance the waste management of batteries – and this is now new and essential – the manufacturers of batteries and accumulators are obliged. How are the tasks of this Directive distributed? – Firstly, the municipalities or community associations, Mrs Lichtenecker, must offer the citizens the free delivery of equipment batteries. Secondly, however, communities have not been obliged to take over old batteries that the trade has collected. Here the manufacturers and dealers have to ensure that the batteries are also disposed of. At the request of the local authorities, the manufacturers of batteries have of course also to take care of the transport of the batteries. They see that this is essentially a matter of removing, re-useing and using batteries accordingly. [[Abg. Oil: Yes, yes!]] The Minister for the Environment is also involved in reviewing these systems. In addition, manufacturers are obliged to set up additional collection sites in each of the political districts in Austria. I think we have never had such a comprehensive set of batteries. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e9850	The next speaker is Mr Steier. Voluntary speaking time limit: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e9872	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! With the AWG-Novelle batteries, we implement EU Directive 2006/66/EC in Austrian law. It aims to improve the environmental balance of batteries and accumulators as well as the activities of all economic operators involved in their life cycle, say manufacturers, distributors and end-users and many others who are directly involved in treatment and recycling. Every year, around 800 000 tonnes of car batteries, 190 000 tonnes of industrial batteries and 160 000 tonnes of equipment batteries are under attack in the EU. Faced with these quantities and heavy metals contained in batteries and accumulators – such as mercury, lead and cadmium – it is absolutely important to minimise harmful effects on the environment and health in waste disposal, if not entirely. The following arrangements are now foreseen for Austria: First, consumers can return batteries free of charge in trade. Secondly, manufacturers and importers of batteries and accumulators must participate in collection and recovery systems and have a share of collection obligation. There is a collection coordination, registration obligations, and collection sites must also be set up. Thirdly, the manufacturers or their collection and recycling systems bear the costs of transporting from the collection site and those of treatment as well as the information of consumers, which is very important. Fourth, the collection of batteries also takes place through the municipalities. Vehicle batteries can be withdrawn at the public waste collection sites of the municipalities, but do not have to. This massively desired derogation for vehicle batteries, in concrete terms the departure from the collection obligation at the municipal waste collection sites, and the only partial involvement of collection coordination, we assess very differentiated. We have therefore urged the Minister of the Environment to pay particular attention to the return possibilities of consumers, to the income situation of the municipalities and to the fair competition of collection and recovery systems and to evaluate the functioning of the system regularly. I should also like to thank the coalition partner very much for the truly consensusal discussion on this matter, and I hope that we have done something here for consumers, which really brings something for the future. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e10293	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Honourable Members! Ladies and gentlemen. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The government template for the revision of the Waste Management Act is now under discussion, in particular, for the implementation of the EU Batteries and Accumulators Directive, as well as old batteries and accumulators. The main objective of this Directive is to minimise the environmental pollution caused by batteries and accumulators and waste batteries. In recent years, the number of electronic and electrical devices with batteries or batteries has increased sharply. The implementation of the Directive makes it clear that producers must bear the costs of the withdrawal system themselves. In Austria, around 2 000 tons of batteries are purchased annually. Low-pollutant alkaline manganese and zinc-coal batteries are the most widely bought batteries. Rien. Some 55 million alkali manganese and zinc-coal batteries, 1.5 million button cells and 3.5 million nickel-cadmium batteries are purchased. Through the recycling of batteries and batteries, valuables such as zinc, iron, nickel and cadmium are recovered. Due to the enormous increase in raw material prices, a completely new situation has also developed in the recycling circuits on the market for waste batteries. Manufacturers or importers of equipment batteries also have to set up a collection site – at least one per political district – where equipment batteries can be delivered. The Minister for the Environment will approve and properly control the establishment of battery collection and recovery systems. In summary, I would like to point out that Austria is certainly at the forefront of the withdrawal and recovery of batteries in Europe. Essential elements of the directive are already reality in Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! Zinc, nickel, lead and cadmium can be dangerous pollutants in the environment – in an endogenous process, on the other hand, expensive value materials. The amendment of the Waste Management Act is therefore to be welcomed to a great extent.

Oillinger: Please greet us!]] The content of the Aarhus Convention deals with the information of the population, better participation and access to courts in the event of the release of genetically modified organisms – and is now expanding by increasing biological safety for consumers. The Contracting Parties are 34 countries with very different standards, such as successor states on the territory of the former Soviet Union, where the economic situation of the environmental idea is often not so far off. For these Contracting Parties, more detailed and detailed rules are therefore applicable than before. It is desirable that the level of public participation legislation increases across the board. For Austria, I would like to say that Article 3(5) of the Convention remains unchanged, which allows it to act in accordance with Austrian legislation, which applies the Austrian Genetic Engineering Act. Thus, a more extensive public participation in the decision-making process is foreseen, as under the Austrian Genetic Engineering Act, any release of genetically modified organisms is subject to a hearing and the corresponding public participation. This Agreement lays down a general obligation on the Parties to ensure, in the area of their existing legislation, that measures lead to proper information for the population and public participation in the decision-making process in the event of deliberate release or planting or the placing on the market of genetically modified organisms. However, the possibility of participation by citizens also means responsibility to participate actively in the process. The SPÖ Group will vote in favour of this amendment for the above reasons.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e11464	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The Aarhus Convention aims to involve the public in environmentally relevant decisions and to allow access to information and courts. It represents an increased involvement of civil society. The Convention itself is a multilateral treaty made up of 55 states within the framework of the Member States. Of these countries, 41 are parties to the Aarhus Convention as of February 2008. Especially in the field of genetic engineering, the public is very interested in participating in decision-making processes. The lack of transparency and the lack of access to information often greatly limit co-design opportunities. Since 2000, an international working group has been working specifically on the topic of genetically modified organisms. Austria, represented by the Federal Environment Agency, was responsible for the management of this working group. The task was to open up opportunities for the concreteisation of the Convention in the field of genetic engineering. Central and Eastern European countries as well as environmental protection organisations were very positive about this development of the Aarhus Convention. A further compromise was reached at the Second Conference in Almaty, Kazakhstan in May 2005. Thus, the Aarhus Convention on decision-making processes in the field of release and product authorisation of genetically modified organisms has been amended. The decision taken in Kazakhstan is an improvement of the rights of the public and contains the elements that have already been quoted very often. Article 6 has been reintroduced and the possible exemption from the public participation procedure in the event of the release, which has already been approved in an analogous way under comparable biogeographical conditions, is not provided for in the Genetic Engineering Act. Article 3(5) shall remain unaffected. On the other hand, however, there is a much stricter law in Austria: § 43 of the Genetic Engineering Act, which regulates the consultation, and finally § 101c, which regulates the genetic engineering register. In summary, therefore, I would like to say that in Austria, under the Genetic Engineering Act, any release of a hearing and a public hearing will be subject. This means that the scheme in Austria is much stricter than the Aarhus Convention. However, I am very confident that the Convention concluded was an essential step towards a joint approach by the 41 parties. This is particularly important for those countries which do not currently have legal regulations. The fact that the project is not completed and is not a result, but that it is planned to hold a new international meeting in 2008 shows that it is a continuous process to further develop this Aarhus Convention. Nevertheless, Mr Mayer has already done so, I would like to make a request to the Federal Minister. In Austria, the question of liability in the field of genetic engineering has not yet been clarified. In my opinion, funds for risk research should be made more available. The result of the Aarhus Convention is a very encouraging result for both the EU and Austria. We voted unanimously in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, and we will do so today.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e14412	Madam President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Mrs. State Secretary! High house! The Court of Auditors gives a good judgment in its report on the security of the electricity supply of the situation in Austria. Austria is one of the countries with the lowest power supply interruption across Europe. However, to ensure that Austria has also grown in future requirements, there is a need for massive investment in the line network. The Court of Auditors notes that the supply of electricity in Austria needs to be safeguarded in a sustainable manner. To this end, it is urgently necessary to complete the 380 kilovolt circuit. This means the completion of the so-called Styria line and the Salzburg line. These lines are of great importance for both safe and reliable power supply. Two structural variants are considered: the transmission line and the partial or complete wiring of the routes. According to the KEMA study, Earth cables and transmission have nearly the same reliability data. I believe that the decision should not lose sight of spatial and nature-protection objectives. The need for the 380 kV ring has been questioned, but in addition to economic considerations, the landscape and health aspects must not be ignored. It is necessary to find a solution that is technically and economically feasible. Short-term profit efforts cannot be the top priority in the field of energy supply. In any case, the additional costs of wiring up the earth must be calculated with the cost of construction land evaluation and the impact on tourism in an overall view. That's why I'm looking. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e14467	Madam President. Members of the Federal Government! Mr President of the Court of Auditors! The Court of Auditors, in its report on electricity security in Austria, sets out in principle: a positive witness. The examination period was 2002 to 2005, and the electricity availability for customers was very good. Austria is one of the countries with the lowest power supply interruption across Europe. Nevertheless, the Court points to a partial threat to security of supply due to low transport capacity in transmission lines. With the 380 kV circuit in Styria and the strengthening of the 220 kV line of St. Peter in Upper Austria in the Innviertel to the Tauern in Salzburg, the current bottlenecks can be eliminated. The report also points to the growing consumption of electricity. From 1995 to 2005, final consumption rose by at least 23 percent, and a continuous increase is also expected for the future. It is therefore recommended by the Court of Auditors that the planned power plant projects in Austria be implemented quickly. As a result, the level of self-production is expected to increase slightly – from 96 to 98 percent. That means, of course, the greatest possible expansion of hydropower. There is actually no alternative to that. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] On the other hand, electricity consumption needs to be countered, for example by improving energy efficiency and raising the awareness of the population for energy saving. For example: Video and TV devices must not be in stand-by mode. It is also the task of policy to draw people's attention to it. The allocation of funds will also take place with the help of loans from the European Investment Bank. So from my point of view, that is not the problem. The key question for “Nabucco” will be: Is it possible to actually fill this “Nabucco” pipeline project with gas? And that's where experts say, like the gas, Head of the RWE: Once “Nabucco” is there, the gas for filling and transport to Europe will also be there. From today's point of view, the most promising thing is now a 10 billion cubic metres supply agreement with the Aseris, with Baku, ultimately with the operators of the gas fields in the Caspian region, in the south of the Caspian Sea. In the medium term, and this was also the purpose of a trip from me to Central Asia, it is also about gas from Turkmenistan, about gas from Kazakhstan. And once the political issues with Iran have been corrected, when the critical question of nuclear enrichment will be corrected, then, of course, Iranian gas is something that can be pumped into Europe by this pipeline. You're right: 30 billion cubic meters are only a relatively small part of the overall needs of the European Union, but I ask you to continue to think. All forecasts say: The European Union's gas demand will grow by 200 to 300 billion cubic metres in the coming years, by 2020, 2030, including because European gas promotion will decline. This concerns, for example, gas from the North Sea. Thus, if you want to do so, these 30 billion cubic meters are just 10 per cent of the additional need of the European Union over the next 20 to 30 years. That's what it's all about! And I have always opposed to playing “Nabucco” against Russian projects à la “Northstream” and “Southstream”. Let me hold you: "Nabucco" is undoubtedly the pipeline project, which is the most advanced of all three projects. Mr Schröder and others in all honors, but this is most advanced. In terms of “Southstream”, it is not at all clear whether you are going through the Black Sea, and if so, at what cost. There will be considerable costs. But once “Southstream” is there, there is nothing against the fact that one also receives gas via “Southstream” and then via “Nabucco” to Central Europe. Why not? These are flexible things, there is nothing ground in stone, there is nothing locked up, but it should be open markets and open pipelines, depending on how we need it. From today’s point of view, Russia is also much less distant from “Nabucco”. It's going to be very slowly accepted. The right thing is: In principle, we want to avoid, of course, a circumvention of Russian territory in Moscow and elsewhere, and it is natural for the gas from the Caspian region to be pumped and flowed through Turkey, Bulgaria, Romania and so on to Central Europe. It is completely new to me – and you have to explain this to me, my colleague – why Austria and taxpayers would then have to pay any allowances of €1.1 billion. I wrote this. This is freely invented and is not at all the subject of material considerations. If we did not meet our CO2 reduction obligations, we would have to pay something for the difference, buy certificates, but that is why it is about pumping 30 billion cubic metres of gas over “Nabucco” to Central Europe and then distributing it through this central gas Hub Baumgarten in Europe and Central Europe, for example to Germany, for example to France and elsewhere. – So of high expertise, apologise for this criticism, this remark and your contribution from my point of view has not really testified. Thank you very much.

We know the problem in Brazil, for example, where more and more large-scale agro-groups are dispersing subsistence farmers from their land, then using and wasting huge areas for agricultural plants, even with the corresponding use of pesticides and fertilizers. ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e17757	We're going to the 16th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are going into the debate. The first speaker is Dr. Sonnberger. 3 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-03-13-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00053_d7e17787	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Today, we are adopting a law that will relieve around 650 000 tenants in Austria by about €35 million. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The rents according to the benchmark law will be made from 1. April 2008 not by 3.6 percent, but by 2.14 percent, by taking the annual average index in the future and not looking from December to December, where we had a very high index of 3.6 percent in December 2007. What does that mean? – This means a savings of €100 a year for the tenant of a rental apartment with a rent of approximately €600 per month. 350 000 tenants are affected positively. In addition, the rental costs of 300,000 flats are reduced according to the housing benefit law, namely for those dwellings where theburger-based reference value is used with the 30 % discount scheme. This means, for example, a savings of approximately €42 a year for a 70-square meter apartment. In autumn, further discussions are being held towards threshold regulation 5 percent, as we already have in the category rents. A threshold of 5 percent means that rents will only be increased again when 5 percent of the price is exceeded since the last rental increase. It is agreed with the SPÖ that, even in the case of such a scheme, the fundamental value-resistance of the rents remains untouchable for constitutional reasons. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Furthermore, we would like to reorganise the cost of the energy card and also the §-3 and §-8 regulations, which have become necessary by the OGH judgment, in terms of maintenance and maintenance. Furthermore, according to Minister Martin Bartenstein, the broker commissions will be made from a maximum of three gross rents to a maximum of two gross rents with likely effectiveness from 1. ==References== Finally, allow me to say: A part of Wilhelm Molterer's package of deinflation has now been implemented, but many tasks are still waiting for us. I don't understand the increase in fees at ORF today. A fee stop would be necessary to suspend the increase in the vignette fee, to strengthen competition in electricity and gas. Please, in Linz the cable rights are reduced by €50, and on the other hand there is an increase in the energy price by twice in the same period. Or: Water and wastewater charges; there are still many areas of action where we can really help citizens effectively. Finally, thank you very much to the Minister of Justice, to the staff of the Ministry of Justice under the very perfect leadership of Dr. Stabentheiner and also to the opposition parties who follow this request.

I therefore welcome the agreement between Federal Minister Bartenstein and Minister of Justice Berger on this new law to be adopted today. This change is an important mosaic in the fight against inflation. This agreement to apply the average annual value of 2.2 per cent instead of 3.6 per cent for the increase in rent is expected to save up to €100 per year for approximately 650 000 households. Minister Bartenstein has also announced that in the course of this year the broker commission will be reduced by law from three to two months' rent. This would be another important step and a relief that would lead to this. My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen, a key factor in housing and rental costs are, of course, energy and operating costs. These are currently the big price drivers. Here, too, it is necessary to do everything in order to avoid further turning on the price screw. I would also consider it particularly useful to move forward with thermal renovation, because this would have a positive effect on energy efficiency. I would like to give my consent to this law.
